# Discoverability Audit

Persistent state for TweetClaw GitHub discoverability, external placement, release hygiene, and no-duplicate outreach. Keep this file compact: preserve current status, blockers, PR/issue links, and next actions; remove repeated heartbeat logs.

## Recurring Rules

- Treat GitHub repo marketability, discoverability, SEO, trust, and quality as a primary recurring objective.
- Before external outreach, read the target README, contribution rules, license, templates, open and closed PRs/issues, and search for `tweetclaw`, `xquik`, `x-twitter-scraper`, and `@xquik/tweetclaw`.
- Treat any existing TweetClaw mention, TweetClaw listing, merged TweetClaw change, or open or closed TweetClaw PR/issue as a duplicate blocker unless a maintainer explicitly asks for follow-up. Xquik-only or `x-twitter-scraper` placement is not a TweetClaw duplicate by itself, but the new contribution must add distinct TweetClaw/OpenClaw plugin or npm package value.
- Prefer one useful repo-native contribution over many thin listings.
- Do not duplicate open PRs, merged PRs, declined submissions, maintainer decisions, or generated catalog entries.
- Keep external PRs narrow, factual, credential-free, placeholder-only, and in the target repo's style.
- Use Markdown body files or stdin with real newlines for external PRs/issues/comments, then read back the body and verify no literal `\n` sequences.
- Check Socket.dev for the latest published npm version before releases and dependency or package-metadata changes. Target 100/100 across Supply Chain Risk, Quality, Maintenance, Vulnerabilities, and License for both the package view and dependency view. If any score drops below 100, treat it as a blocker until the offending package or dependency is removed, replaced, or explicitly documented with a concrete mitigation and replacement plan.
- If a target exposes secret-like examples, prefer a narrow safety patch or private disclosure path and never repeat the value.
- For generated catalogs, identify the upstream source before opening direct generated-output PRs.
- Every heartbeat run must check broken links across TweetClaw public surfaces and the current target, fix stale public URLs when appropriate, and record blockers here before committing.
- Current automation cadence is 5 minutes. The automation prompt includes the NHS Agentic Readiness badge rule for relevant handled badge areas and the explicit exception to never add it to Apify READMEs or Apify actor docs.
- Context7 work is paused by user request. Do not monitor or update Context7 unless the user explicitly asks later.
- Compact this audit whenever repeated run history starts hiding the current action state.

## Current Compatibility Findings

- OpenClaw npm latest and beta are both `2026.5.7` as of 2026-05-09 11:05 UTC.
- OpenClaw `v2026.5.7` is now the default latest GitHub release, published at `2026-05-07T20:57:43Z`. Compare `v2026.5.6...v2026.5.7` showed 76 commits focused on release hardening, managed plugin dependency handling, channel commands, cron/task repair, and package metadata, with no required TweetClaw manifest, schema, or route change.
- Official OpenClaw manifest docs still place native plugin discovery metadata in `openclaw.plugin.json` and install-on-demand metadata in `package.json#openclaw.install`.
- OpenClaw plugin build docs still support published package installs. TweetClaw uses npm-first install metadata while the ClawHub listing lags, so new docs and outreach should not describe TweetClaw as ClawHub-first.
- Official OpenClaw package-entrypoint docs require native npm plugins to declare `openclaw.extensions`, ship built JavaScript through matching `openclaw.runtimeExtensions`, and pass `pluginApi` and `minGatewayVersion` compatibility checks. Official dependency-resolution docs also say plugins that import `openclaw/plugin-sdk/*` declare `openclaw` as a peer dependency while OpenClaw skips npm peer materialization and reasserts host links in managed installs. TweetClaw imports `openclaw/plugin-sdk/plugin-entry`, so the `openclaw` package must remain a required peer rather than an optional peer.
- GitHub docs continue to frame README files and topics as primary repo discovery and evaluation surfaces.
- Xquik public `llms.txt` and billing page say MPP covers 31 X-API read-only endpoints as of 2026-05-08 22:42 UTC. The billing page says media downloads require API key or session-cookie authentication because they create gallery links. The API overview regex check produced one false-positive `32` hit from rendered style text, not an MPP endpoint statement. Local Xquik source remains source of truth: `lib/mpp/pricing.test.ts` asserts 31 eligible routes and `POST /api/v1/x/media/download` is not MPP eligible; TweetClaw 1.6.25 clarifies that user media MPP means timeline reads, not media file download.

## Audited No-Action Or Blocked Candidates

- openclaw/skills: public search and directory snippets still reference this as an official skills source, but `gh repo view openclaw/skills` did not resolve on 2026-05-18, so no GitHub-native TweetClaw contribution path was available.
- EthanYolo01/Awesome-OpenClaw: rechecked repo metadata on 2026-05-18 after fresh search results. Existing PR 6 already covers TweetClaw, so no duplicate issue or PR was opened.
- nitzzzu/openclaw-skills-explorer: cloned and checked README, source layout, missing license state, and empty open and closed duplicate issue and PR searches on 2026-05-18. The project builds and scans a catalog from the OpenClaw skills registry rather than accepting one-off external tool listings, so no direct TweetClaw placement was opened.
- filiksyos/mcptoskill: cloned and checked README, source layout, package metadata, no repo-level license file, and empty open and closed duplicate issue and PR searches on 2026-05-18. The project converts arbitrary remote MCP servers into OpenClaw or Hermes skills, while TweetClaw is already a native OpenClaw npm plugin; a TweetClaw converter example would be low-context without maintainer request.
- openclaw/nix-openclaw: checked repo metadata, license state, and public search result on 2026-05-18. The repo packages OpenClaw for Nix and is not an OpenClaw plugin catalog, skill directory, MCP registry, or X/Twitter automation guide, so no TweetClaw placement was opened.
- OnlyTerp/openclaw-optimization-guide: checked README, CONTRIBUTING, AWESOME, Part 23, Part 20, MIT license, repo metadata, open and closed issues and PRs, duplicate TweetClaw/Xquik/x-twitter-scraper searches, and local duplicate terms on 2026-05-18. No TweetClaw duplicate was found, but the contribution guide rejects marketing copy and the awesome list requires resources the maintainer has used on a production OpenClaw deployment or seen solve a real problem, so no direct TweetClaw listing PR was opened.
- wende/openclaw-skills-as-tools: checked repo metadata on 2026-05-18. The repo has no detected license and is a generic auto-discovery bridge for local `skills/**/plugin.ts` agent tools, not a public skill catalog or X/Twitter workflow guide, so no TweetClaw placement was opened.

## Package And Release Notes

- Keep future package versions newer than npm latest before publishing package-affecting safety, compatibility, docs-in-package, or metadata fixes.
- Use `npm run check:all`, `npm pack --dry-run --json`, and `node scripts/clawpack.mjs dry-run` before releases.
- Do not run ClawHub publish again until the `@xquik/tweetclaw` owner record is migrated or ClawHub grants a scope exception. Keep `node scripts/clawpack.mjs dry-run` as the artifact check before npm releases.
- After any future ClawHub publish, inspect latest and version-specific package JSON, request a rescan, and record artifact kind, digest metadata, scan status, source repo/ref/commit, and blockers in `docs/clawpack-release.md`.
- Do not publish a renamed `xquik` plugin alias from a TweetClaw artifact unless the publisher explicitly approves a real alias package identity.

## Hygiene Notes

- `.gitignore` covers env files, registry tokens, local OpenClaw/ClawHub/Context7 state, package archives, dependency/build output, logs, caches, local agents, editors, and security scan artifacts.
- `scripts/check-versions.mjs` guards version sync and protected public wording without publishing the blocked terms.
- Public hygiene scans should cover Git-tracked plus non-ignored untracked files.
- Use file paths and counts when reporting potential secret-like local files; never print file contents.
